# Changelog

All notable changes to Affinidi Agent Gateway will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

## [1.0.9] - 2026-11-12

### Breaking

- **Refuse an external consent connect that brings no refresh token** — If a vendor issues access tokens that expire without a refresh token, set the provider's `token_refresh_enabled` to false, or ask the vendor for offline access in the authorization request (`offline_access` at Entra, `access_type=offline` at Google): otherwise its connects now fail. For a provider with `token_refresh_enabled`, completing a connect whose token answer has an access token that expires but no refresh token fails with the reason `no_refresh_token`, nothing is stored, and the access token is revoked at the vendor when the provider has a revocation endpoint. Such a grant could never be refreshed, so the person would have been asked to connect again as soon as it expired. An access token without an expiry, and every answer for a provider with `token_refresh_enabled` set to false, are kept as before.
- **Report an invalid managed identity as identity_validation_failed** — An agent card, message response, stream event or MCP response whose managed identity is present but invalid was refused with identity_extension_missing. It is now refused with identity_validation_failed, still 422: an agent-identity/v1 declared without its payload, an identity payload with no identity fields, and, on a public agent card, an agent-identity-credential/v1 whose did is not its presentation's holder and subject or whose presentation is missing or malformed. identity_extension_missing is kept for an identity that was not sent, so callers and operators can tell a missing identity from a bad one. A caller that matches on code identity_extension_missing to detect these cases must also match identity_validation_failed.
- **⭐ Refuse failed callers on legacy Access Points with source authentication** — In v1.0.7 and earlier, a legacy surface, one stored without access_point.public (created before v1.0.4, built from DynamoDB, restored from an older backup or edited by hand, and never closed or made public since), handed a caller whose credential was missing or invalid to policy when it requires caller authentication without on_failure: reject, an inbound from_jwt_claim identity or protected resource metadata, so unless a policy denied input.source_auth.method == "failed" the request reached the Target. Such a caller is now refused with 401 before policy and the Target, as on a closed Access Point, on direct inbound, proxy:// MCP Targets, a2a-proxy:// Targets and Fabric receive. Only a public Access Point (access_point.public true) still hands failed callers to policy. Callers with a valid credential, public and closed surfaces, and legacy surfaces without caller authentication are unaffected. Callers that stop working are clients with a revoked, expired or wrong key or token, callers that send no credential, anonymous callers a policy admitted on purpose, and Fabric partners whose sending surface checks the same credential header. Before upgrading, find them as auth_failed audit events with blocking false on surfaces stored without public (select auth_failed under Settings, VP Audit to record them); after upgrading, the gateway logs a warning at start for each legacy surface that now refuses them. To keep admitting failed callers, mark the surface public (the Public access switch, or a public attribute of true on a DynamoDB item). Nothing is written to the stored surface, so a rollback to v1.0.7 or earlier loads it unchanged and reopens it; a surface closed with public false before upgrading keeps refusing failed callers after a rollback to v1.0.4 to v1.0.7. Like a closed Access Point's, these refusals return before the surface rate limit, so repeated wrong credentials no longer count against it. The dashboard's legacy notice now says failed callers are refused. See "Legacy Access Points after upgrading" in docs/SOURCE_AUTH.md.

### Security

- **Ship the container image on a slim runtime base without build tools** — The runtime image is now built from a digest-pinned debian:bookworm-slim with only the shared libraries, xmlsec1 and bootstrap CLIs the gateway needs, instead of the Rust build image. It no longer contains compilers, development packages or git, which removes most of the scanner findings the larger image carried.
- **Scan the Dockerfile and container image for vulnerabilities in CI** — Every container build now scans the Dockerfile for misconfigurations and the built image for fixable high and critical vulnerabilities and embedded secrets, and lists the findings in the job summary. A finding blocks the run, so a release image with a fixable finding never gets a version tag, unless the TRIVY_ENFORCE repository variable is set to false. The boot probe also checks for missing shared libraries.
- **Publish a release only from a pull request opened by the release app** — The release gate now requires the merged release pull request to be authored by the release app, not by any bot account, and the tag push passes the app token to git through its environment instead of the remote URL, so it no longer appears in a process listing.
- **⭐ Confirm the caller at their identity provider before legacy consent** — A legacy authorization code credential provider can now carry a consent_confirmation setting that names a login client at the callers' identity provider, whose client ID and secret are both read from the secrets store in the provider's own tenant, and the JWT verification strategy that identifies them. When a JWT caller whose issuer and principal claim are that strategy's opens a consent link, the gateway first signs them in at the identity provider, verifies the ID token and checks that it names the same person the link was issued to, and only then continues to the vendor. Another person is refused with 403 before the vendor is reached, on a page that tells them to switch account at the identity provider. Saving the setting checks the provider type, the strategy and its tenant, both endpoints, the client ID and secret, the confirmation redirect URI, the principal claim and the identity hash pepper. A setting that no longer confirms any caller, for example after the pepper is lost on a redeploy, refuses consent links to JWT callers. Consent links of providers without the setting, of API key, mTLS and DID callers, and of Transit Point and Fabric callers are minted as before unless the provider sets require, with a warning in the gateway log when the provider has the setting.

### Added

- **Let the operator permit tenant-specific issuers by pattern** — The new `external_consent_issuer_patterns` of gateway.json, set only by the operator's file, permits issuers that differ only by the customer's tenant, such as `https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenant}/v2.0`, without listing each one in `external_consent_issuers`. Each pattern has exactly one `{tenant}`, a whole path segment after a fixed host or the leftmost label of a fixed host, and says whether a UUID or a DNS label may stand for it, so the value can never reach into another segment, label or host. A pattern can also list the endpoint and resource origins a matching issuer may use. Provider create, surface save, the connect API and every grant release and refresh check patterns beside the exact list, and a pattern list that is set, even empty, counts as the operator's list for a tenant's provider. A pattern that is not valid permits nothing and is named at startup, and while patterns are set the credential policy report, at startup and on every reload, warns about each stored external consent provider neither list permits. A host label pattern's fixed host must be one under which only the vendor creates labels, never a shared hosting or public suffix. It is read once, at startup, and a configuration reload keeps it. Without the setting nothing changes.
- **Let an external consent provider leave resource out of its token requests** — An external consent provider takes a new `send_resource` setting, `true` by default. Created with `false`, its authorization code exchange and every refresh of its grants send no `resource` parameter to the token endpoint, so a token endpoint that refuses it, such as the Microsoft identity platform v2.0 one, can still issue grants. Its tokens then carry no RFC 8707 audience binding, their audience following the scopes alone, so it is meant only for such a token endpoint. The provider's `resource` stays required and still decides the only origin its grants are sent to. The setting is fixed for the provider's life: an update that sends another value is refused with `provider_field_immutable`, and a provider without an issuer cannot turn it off. The dashboard no longer warns about an Entra v2.0 resource for an external consent provider that leaves it out, and tells one that sends it to be re-created with `send_resource` set to false.
- **Publish GitHub Releases with a prebuilt Linux binary** — A manual Prepare release workflow bumps the version, builds the changelog and opens a reviewed release pull request. Merging it tags the commit and creates a GitHub Release whose notes are the reviewed changelog text, with the agent-gateway binary, a SHA256 checksum and a build-provenance attestation.
- **Publish a multi-arch container image to GHCR** — Each release tag builds linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 images, boot-probes both, and publishes one multi-arch manifest to ghcr.io/affinidi-ai/agent-gateway tagged X.Y.Z, X.Y and latest, with an attested build provenance.
- **Let a surface allow browser pages from its own origins** — A surface can list up to 20 exact https origins in browser_origins. A cross-origin request, preflight included, to a path of that surface is then allowed from those origins as well as from network.cors, so a page a surface owner hosts on their own domain can call the surface with no gateway.json edit and no restart. An origin listed on one surface gets nothing on another, credentials are never allowed cross-origin, and a saved change applies from the next request. Origins are validated and stored in normalised form when the surface is saved.
- **Give a surface's Target a stable per-caller key for each verified JWT caller** — A surface takes an optional `caller_key` naming a secret in the secrets store by `secret_id`, with a `header` (`x-caller-key` by default) and ordered `identity_claims` (`["sub"]` by default). On each request whose caller presented a JWT the gateway verified on that request, the Target receives HKDF-SHA256 of the secret, salted with `agent-gateway-caller-key-v2`, as 32 bytes of base64url without padding, so a Target that keeps data per caller can encrypt it with a key its own operator cannot derive. The info binds the key to the surface, to the key source that verified the token (the strategy's JWKS URI as configured, or the SHA-256 of the canonical inline key set), to the audience claim and the sorted accepted audiences, and to the token's `iss` and the first identity claim it carries as a string, each field length-prefixed. So editing a strategy to trust another key set, or adding an audience, yields keys no caller had rather than an existing caller's key; it also changes every caller's key, by design. API key, DID Auth, mTLS and anonymous callers, and callers whose credential failed, get none. When the secret cannot be read the request is forwarded without the header and a warning names the surface. A save refuses a header outside the caller key family (`x-caller-key`, or `x-caller-key-` followed by lower-case words, at most 64 characters) or one the surface already uses, invalid claims, and a secret of fewer than 32 bytes; a PUT that leaves the setting out, as a Surface Builder save does, keeps it. Log redaction treats `caller_key` fields and attributes as credentials.
- **Set consent confirmation in the dashboard's credential provider editor** — The credential provider editor of a legacy authorization code provider now has a Consent confirmation switch with the login client ID and client secret, both picked from the secrets store, the authorization and token endpoints, JWT verification strategy and optional ID-token principal claim, offering only strategies the provider's tenant can use, and shows the Confirmation redirect URI to register at the identity provider. Saving sends the setting while the switch is on and clears a stored one when it is switched off.
- **Refuse consent links to callers consent confirmation cannot confirm** — consent_confirmation.require, off by default and set by the editor's Refuse callers that can't be confirmed switch, refuses the request and mints no consent link for a caller the setting cannot confirm, such as an API key, mTLS or DID caller, a Transit Point or Fabric request, or a caller of another issuer, instead of a link that goes straight to the vendor. Use it on a provider whose callers can all be confirmed.
- **Count consent links of providers with consent confirmation** — The Prometheus counter agent_gateway_consent_confirmation_links_total counts every consent link a provider with consent confirmation is asked for, by provider, surface, outcome (confirmed, unconfirmed or refused) and reason, so operators can see how many links skip the confirmation rather than only the first log line.

### Changed

- **Wait seven days before proposing new dependency versions** — Dependabot now opens version update pull requests only for releases that have been published for at least seven days. Security updates are not delayed.
- **Stop forwarding any x-caller-key header a caller sends, on every surface** — Every header whose name, lower-cased with `_` read as `-`, is `x-caller-key` or starts with `x-caller-key-` is now dropped from what a caller, a managed agent or a peer gateway sends, on every surface and path (Access Point, mirror, Fabric send and receive, Transit Point, agent card fetch and MCP proxy backend), whether or not the surface sets `caller_key`, so no caller can supply or spoof a caller key and no surface's choice of header affects another's traffic. A Target that read such a header from its callers must take it under another name.
- **Refuse deleting a JWT verification strategy a credential provider uses** — DELETE /api/v1/jwt-verification-strategies/{id} now answers 409, listing the providers, while a credential provider's consent_confirmation.jwt_verification_strategy_id or its consent_identity_strategy_id names the strategy, so deleting it no longer breaks modern consent and can't leave consent confirmation without its strategy. This also applies to providers that never set consent_confirmation. The Strategies tab's alert names those providers, counts the ones the user can't view, and says that a provider's consent confirmation is changed in its editor and its consent_identity_strategy_id through the API.
- **Refuse a strategy issuer that a credential provider using it would refuse** — PUT /api/v1/jwt-verification-strategies/{id} now answers 409, listing the providers, when it changes expected_issuer to one that a credential provider naming the strategy would refuse at its own save: an issuer of this gateway's own STS or an Entra issuer while callers are named by sub for consent_confirmation, or an issuer that is not a canonical https URL for consent_identity_strategy_id. Before, such an edit stopped modern consent from finding the strategy. The strategy editor shows the refusal with those providers and where to change them.
- **Reword the start-up log line about a surface that refuses failed callers** — The warning logged at start for a surface stored without a Public access setting that authenticates callers now says it is configured for authenticated callers only, that a request without valid credentials is denied with 401, and that switching the Access Point to Public access allows those callers.

### Fixed

- **⭐ Serve the public agent card when the managed agent publishes no identity** — A surface with managed identity refused its public agent card with 422 identity_extension_missing when the managed agent's card had no identity extension, as a Microsoft Foundry agent's has not, so the agent could not be discovered. The gateway now serves that card without an identity credential, with every other change applied, and logs a warning once per surface. This applies to A2A and AP2 surfaces, and only to a JSON object with a string name whose extensions list neither identity extension in any letter case. A card whose identity is present but invalid, that carries an identity credential the gateway cannot use, whose extensions are not a list, or that is not a named card is still refused, and messages, streams and extended cards without an identity are still refused.
- **Show a card served without a managed identity on the Agent Cards view** — The agent card preview reports a card served without an identity credential as fetch status served_without_identity with cause identity_missing, and keeps it as the last good card. The Agent Cards view shows "Served – No managed identity" with a warning that the managed agent's card has no identity extension, and the Managed Agent panel's fetch line shows the same status in the warning tone, so operators see why the card carries no identity credential instead of a failure.
- **Name why the gateway will not attach the managed identity to the agent card** — The agent card preview adds fetch.identity_error beside the identity_failed cause: the identity error code the card route answers with, or credential_injection_failed when the gateway could not sign or attach its own credential, and fetch.identity_slot, which names the slot that failed (protected_identity or external_identity). The Agent Cards view names that cause and what to do, pointing at the external Identity element when the external slot failed, instead of only asking the operator to check the gateway logs.
- **Show a surface's page again instead of a blank page** — Opening a surface in the dashboard showed a blank page, because the Trust Check element's code read two of its own values before they were defined in the built dashboard. They are now defined before anything reads them, and the surface page loads again.
- **Report a caller JWT without an iss claim as an invalid issuer** — A JWT Bearer caller whose token has no iss claim is now refused with the reason Invalid issuer instead of Invalid audience, in the gateway log and in the cause a consent confirmation logs for an ID token. The response and the audit record are unchanged.
- **Log a DynamoDB public attribute the gateway ignored, also at start** — A surface built from DynamoDB takes access_point.public from an optional public attribute on its item, a boolean or the string true or false. Any other value, such as yes, True or a number, is ignored and the surface keeps no Public access setting, so it refuses failed callers. The warning saying so was raised while the DynamoDB configuration was read, before the gateway installs its log subscriber, so it never reached the log at start and an operator whose opt-out had no effect got no reason. The gateway now holds such warnings until logging starts and logs them then, naming the item and the type of value it held, never the value, and logs later ones at once.
- **Answer route misses with a protocol-neutral 404** — A request on a surface route that no active surface serves now gets a 404 application/problem+json body with type about:blank and code: no_route, whose detail names the path and says to check the entry point URL and that the surface is saved and enabled. The body is the same for disabled, deleted and unknown surfaces.
- **Answer Transit Point and MCP Proxy misses with a neutral 404** — A Transit Point path that no enabled Transit Point serves, and an entry point whose MCP Proxy is missing, now get a 404 application/problem+json body with type about:blank and code: no_route instead of an A2A error type. The body is the same whether Outbound is off, the alias is missing or the alias is unknown.
- **Drop the A2A error type from the MCP batch refusal** — A JSON-RPC batch sent to an MCP surface still gets a 422 application/problem+json answer, but its type is now about:blank instead of an A2A protocol URL, and it carries code: batch_not_supported so callers can tell it from other 422 answers.
- **Let the chosen release type decide the next version again** — Prepare release refused a patch or minor release while a pending changelog fragment had a breaking or added entry. The release type chosen when the workflow is started decides the version again, whatever categories the fragments use. A run that cannot work out the next version now stops at that step with its own error instead of failing later on an empty version in Cargo.toml.
- **Stop env preparation when a prefix shadows a dashboard page** — scripts/_prepare.sh, and so make config-debug, run-debug and run-watch, stops with an error naming each channel, MCP proxy, proxy route or redirect route prefix in gateway.json whose first path segment is a dashboard page. The gateway's own startup behaviour is unchanged.
- **⭐ Start on storage without hard links, such as an Azure Files SMB share** — The transit token signing key was created by hard-linking a complete copy into place, which an SMB share refuses with "Operation not supported", so a gateway whose vc_keys directory sits on Azure Files stopped on its first start after upgrading and on any new deployment. When that link fails, the gateway now logs a warning and creates the key file exclusively instead, filling it in place, so instances starting together still create one key between them, and an instance reading the file while another fills it waits for it to be complete. No configuration is needed.
- **⭐ Recover from an empty transit token key file after an interrupted start** — Where the storage volume has no hard links, the gateway creates the transit token key file and fills it in place. A first start killed in between left the file empty, and every later start then refused it after waiting 5 seconds for a creator to finish, so the deployment stayed down until an operator deleted the file. An empty key file is now removed and a new key created in its place; a key file with any content in it, even damaged, is still never replaced, and the error naming it now says it is empty and what to do about it. An instance whose own key file is removed while it fills it reads the file back, finds the key that replaced it and uses that one, so the instances still share a key. Only an instance that found the file unchanged across those 5 seconds removes it, and an instance creating the key removes nothing, so a file another instance is filling is left alone. The removal renames the file aside before deleting it and puts it back if it turns out to hold a key, so a creator that was merely slow keeps its own file, and a creation that cannot fill its file deletes only the file it created. Instances recovering the same empty file together end on one key, and one that finds the key gone while it is creating it reads it again rather than stopping.
- **Wait out a transit token key file that cannot be read yet** — An instance reading the key file while another filled it waited only for content that did not parse. With encryption at rest on, a file caught half written comes back as a failed decryption, and a share that drops a read as a read error, and both stopped the start at once instead of waiting. Those failures are now retried for the same 5 seconds, and the start that gives up still reports the real cause.
- **Warn when a volume keeps a secret file readable beyond its owner** — An SMB share mounted without file_mode=0600 accepts the permission change the gateway makes on a key file without applying it. The gateway now reads the mode back and warns that the file is still readable beyond its owner, instead of logging that it restricted it, and the deployment requirements state the mount options to give such a share.
- **List the gateway's signing key under assertionMethod in its DID document** — The gateway signs the credentials it issues with its Ed25519 Multikey (#key-2), but the DID document it published when first started listed only #key-1 under assertionMethod, so strict Data Integrity verifiers rejected every credential the gateway signed. A new gateway now lists both keys under assertionMethod, and the document is built in one place so the published and generated copies cannot differ. A gateway that already published its DID log keeps that document.

### Removed

- **Remove the unset Public access note and Close button from the dashboard** — The Access Point panel no longer shows the note under the Public access switch for a surface stored without that setting, nor its Close Access Point button. The gateway's own warnings about such a surface are unchanged. Set Public access or access_point.public to choose.

## [1.0.8] - 2026-11-11

### Breaking

- **⭐ Name the provider's id, not its slug, in consent_granted audit records** — The OAuth callback's consent_granted record now holds the credential provider's record id in provider_id, where it held the provider's provider_id slug. This applies in the VP Audit Log and in what Governance Audit integrations receive. A consumer that matched these records by slug must match the provider's id. Records written by earlier releases keep the slug.
- **⭐ Refuse delegation records and logs to tokens scoped by required headers alone** — An access token scoped by required headers alone, or by a resource pattern without a tenant, read every tenant's agent-keyed vault tokens and delegation audit records on GET /api/v1/delegation-vault, GET /api/v1/delegation-vault/{id}, GET /api/v1/delegation-audit and GET /v1/audit, and one scoped by required headers alone could revoke any tenant's tokens. Such a token now reads none of them: lists and audit pages are empty and every token answers 404. DELETE /api/v1/delegation-vault/{id} and DELETE /api/v1/delegation-vault/by-user/{user_hash} now answer 403 to every caller but a session or an appliance-wide access token, and keep the tokens, and GET /v1/logs/download answers 403 to such a token. Automation that read or revoked these records, or downloaded the logs, with such a token needs an appliance-wide access token, or a tenant's token to read its own tenant's records.

### Security

- **Refuse a new client secret or a retype on a stored http token endpoint** — In warn mode a stored credential provider keeps a plain http token endpoint that insecure_token_endpoints does not list. An update that sets a JWT Bearer provider's client_authentication to client_secret_basic or client_secret_post, when it sent no client secret before, or that changes the provider's type to an OAuth type, is now refused with "token_endpoint must be an https URL", so neither a new client authentication nor a retype starts sending a client secret in clear text. Setting none, clearing the setting, or switching between the two secret methods is still saved, and listing the endpoint allows it.
- **Refuse an update that clears a credential provider's client references** — An update to an oauth2_client_credentials or oauth2_authorization_code provider must now leave it with its token endpoint, client ID reference and client secret reference, as a create does, and an update to an api_key provider with its API key reference. An update that sends an empty client reference, or retypes a provider to one of these types without the references it needs, is refused with the same 400 message as a create. A provider that keeps a plain http token endpoint can no longer have its client secret cleared and set again by a later update, which would have made it start sending the secret in clear text.
- **Mark the Authorization header of a revocation request as sensitive** — A revocation request that authenticates its client by HTTP Basic now marks its Authorization header sensitive, as a token request already does, so no debug output of the request shows the client's credentials.
- **Stop a compressed agent card from pointing callers past a Transit Point** — A Transit Point returned a gzip-compressed agent card unrewritten, so its URL named the agent itself and callers that followed it skipped the Transit Point's policy, authentication, rate limits and audit. The card is now fetched uncompressed and a card compressed all the same is refused with 502.
- **⭐ Refuse to delete an Authority that Trust Checks or Trust Recorder entries use** — DELETE /v1/authorities/{id} now answers 409 while a Trust Check or Trust Recorder entry on a surface (base or variant) or a managed connection names the Authority's DID, listing the surfaces and managed connections the caller may view with the Trust Checks and Trust Recorder entries that name it, and counting the rest; records of a tenant that may not use the Authority do not count. Before, the Authority was deleted and the checks kept passing. force=true deletes it anyway only when the caller may view every record that names it, and records an authority_force_deleted event in the VP Audit Log. Moving an Authority, a surface or a managed connection to a tenant that would make such a link cross-tenant is refused. The dashboard lists what uses the Authority in a dialog.
- **Fail Trust Checks and stop Trust Recorder entries whose Authority is gone** — Trust Checks and Trust Recorder entries now record in authority_source whether their DID is linked to an Authority or found among the Issuers. The gateway sets it on every save and on load and never drops a link to an Authority. A Trust Check linked to an Authority that no longer exists fails with AUTHORITY_NOT_FOUND without querying its trust registry, and a Trust Recorder entry in that state writes nothing and shows the recorder reason authority_not_found, instead of both carrying on under a DID nothing vouches for. A save linking to such an Authority, or to another tenant's, is refused with 400, and the Surface Builder marks the element "Configuration required" and blocks Save until it names another Authority or is removed. A record that named an Authority deleted before this release has no link to it and keeps running; each load logs the DIDs a surface or managed connection names that are in neither the Authorities nor the Issuers register, so it can be found and fixed.
- **Hide internal source authentication errors from 500 response bodies** — A caller that hit an internal source authentication failure received the underlying error text in a 500 body. The body now carries a generic message only, and the full error is logged on the gateway.
- **Refuse STS tokens of another tenant's connection at JWT Bearer surfaces** — A JWT Bearer surface that trusts this gateway's STS now accepts a token the STS issued only when the managed connection it was minted for belongs to the surface's tenant or to no tenant. Every STS access token and ID-JAG now names that connection by its record id in a `connection_id` claim, so renaming a connection or reusing its `client_id` cannot move a token to another connection; a token minted before this change has no such claim and is refused at these surfaces, so callers must exchange for a new one. A token minted for another tenant's connection, or for a connection that no longer exists, is refused as an invalid credential, so one tenant can no longer reach another tenant's JWT Bearer surface with a token minted for its audience. A token signed under the gateway DID that names no managed connection in `client_id`, such as an AP2 presentation, is refused there too, and every token of this gateway's issuers fails as an internal error while the managed connection store is unavailable. MCP resource tokens (`mcp_http.authorization`) get the same check on surfaces, over Fabric and on MCP Proxies, so a token of a deleted connection or of another tenant's connection is refused there.
- **Show a tenant's access token only its own tenant's delegation vault tokens** — GET /api/v1/delegation-vault and GET /api/v1/delegation-vault/{id} returned every tenant's agent-keyed tokens, as metadata, to a tenant's access token. A vault token now belongs to its credential provider's tenant: a tenant's access token lists only its own tenant's, and reading another answers 404 like a missing token. Tokens of an appliance-wide, missing or deleted provider are shown only to appliance-wide callers, since an appliance-wide provider can hold any tenant's callers' tokens. Sessions and appliance-wide access tokens still read every token.
- **Show a tenant's access token only its own tenant's delegation audit records** — GET /api/v1/delegation-audit and GET /v1/audit returned the whole audit log to a tenant's access token. It now gets only records whose credential provider belongs to its tenant, and totals, category counts, dropped_records and the text filter cover only those; a line that does not parse names no provider and is counted only for appliance-wide callers, who alone are also told the size of the whole history. Records without a provider, such as policy decisions, and records of an appliance-wide, missing or deleted provider are shown only to appliance-wide callers. Dashboard sessions and appliance-wide access tokens still read the whole log.
- **Keep store error detail out of delegation vault error answers** — The delegation vault's list, read and revoke routes and its revoke of a user's tokens answered a failed store read or revoke with the store's error text, which can quote storage file paths. They now answer a fixed body, such as {"error":"Failed to revoke"}, and log the detail instead. The revokes log it escaped, since it can quote the id the caller sent.
- **Refuse the server log download to a tenant's access token** — GET /v1/logs/download served the server logs, which hold every tenant's request and credential delegation detail, to any caller with audit.view, a tenant's access token or one scoped by a resource pattern included. It now answers such a token 403; sessions and appliance-wide access tokens still download the logs. A failed download answers {"error":"Failed to download logs"} instead of an error naming the log directory, and the detail is logged.

### Added

- **Let a JWT Bearer provider identify its OAuth client to the token endpoint** — A JWT Bearer credential provider can set client_authentication to none (a public client), client_secret_basic or client_secret_post, and its grant then carries the client ID, or the client ID and secret, by HTTP Basic or in the form body, and its scopes in the scope form field, which Hydra grants scopes from. Servers such as Ory Hydra refuse a grant that names no client. A provider that sets no client authentication sends the same client-less grant as before, whatever client references it stores, so stored providers behave as they did. Create and update refuse a method without the client references it sends and a client authentication on any other provider type. An empty client ID reference clears it on a JWT Bearer provider, so the dashboard's Not sent leaves no reference that blocks deleting its secret. The provider list shows each provider's client authentication, and the dashboard's credential provider editor offers the choice.
- **⭐ Raise user.login_failed for failed console sign-ins and name the login method** — A new integration event, user.login_failed, is raised when a passkey sign-in reaches credential verification and fails, and when a SAML response is rejected, so a SIEM or Agent Watch can see failed sign-ins. It carries LOGIN_METHOD (passkey or saml) and FAILURE_REASON (invalid_credential, not_approved, unknown_user or saml_rejected), and the user variables only when the account exists, taken from the stored account; the username a caller typed never leaves the appliance. user.login now carries LOGIN_METHOD too. The answer a failed sign-in gets is unchanged. Failed sign-in deliveries are held to 10 of the 100 background tasks, so a flood of failed sign-ins cannot starve other events. See docs/INTEGRATIONS.md.

### Fixed

- **Keep identity rotation history after a rollback or an interrupted write** — An identity record saved by a release that does not track rotation_count, as after a rollback to 1.0.6, no longer has a readable rotation history overwritten by its next rotation: that rotation keeps the history on file and adds to it, without recording the same rotation twice unless the credential rotated again before a failed save was retried, which leaves one extra version, and fails instead when the history cannot be loaded because of a disk or key service failure. Such an identity's history shows again only after that rotation. The history is now written through a temporary file, so a write interrupted from now on leaves the earlier history instead of a torn file that fails every later rotation of that identity. A history file torn before this change still needs restoring or removing; on a record saved by 1.0.6, its next rotation replaces it. See docs/SOURCE_AUTH.md.
- **Show the right changes in Version History while it reloads rotations** — When a credential rotated while Version History was open, the Identities page compared the earlier rotation list with the updated row until the new list arrived, showing the newest change against the wrong rotation. It now shows the loading state until then.
- **Stop loading rotation pages when Version History closes** — Closing Version History or collapsing its row while the rotations load now aborts the remaining page requests instead of fetching every page and discarding the result. When one identity's rotations fail to load, the other identities' page requests now stop too.
- **Serve A2A agent cards and Fabric replies from agents that gzip their answers** — A caller's Accept-Encoding gzip reached the agent, whose compressed answer the gateway could not read. Access Point card discovery failed with 502 and Fabric carried a corrupted body. Agent card fetches and every Fabric forward now ask the agent for an uncompressed answer, and one compressed all the same is refused with 502 rather than corrupted.
- **Name a compressed agent card as the cause of a failed card fetch** — An agent that compresses its card although the gateway asked for none was reported to operators as "Not an agent card" and to Transit Point callers as "Failed to read upstream response". The Agent Cards view now shows "Card compressed" with what to fix, Transit Points answer "Upstream response is compressed", and the refusal counts as a failed call.
- **Persist audiences entered in Caller Context** — Save an audience typed into the Caller Context editor even when the operator does not press Add, Enter, or comma first, including when the operator saves with Cmd/Ctrl+S while the field is focused. The audience now appears in the list and clears the warning that audience claim validation is disabled.
- **Return 401 when the caller JWT is missing on from_jwt_claim surfaces** — An Access Point whose inbound identity is from_jwt_claim answered 500 when a caller sent no bearer token or an invalid one, on direct and fabric paths. It now refuses such a caller with 401 before gateway policy and never calls the target. The body says only that the caller credential is missing or invalid; the reason is logged, not sent.
- **Refuse from_jwt_claim surfaces unless jwt_bearer is their only caller method** — Saving a surface whose inbound identity, protected identity or Transit Point identity is from_jwt_claim now requires jwt_bearer as its only caller authentication method. A surface with no method, or with another method beside jwt_bearer, can never supply the JWT claims the identity reads, so it is refused at save time instead of answering 500 per request. Stored surfaces that break the rule still load with a warning.
- **Fix flaky credential provider delete test race condition** — The a_provider_delete_dropped_part_way_still_revokes_what_was_deferred test waited only for the first revocation token to arrive, then immediately asserted both tokens were present. In CI with different timing, only the second token arrived before the assertion, causing intermittent failures. Now explicitly waits for both tokens before asserting.
- **Show a tenant's access token the consents its own providers recorded** — A tenant's access token never read its own tenant's consent_granted records from the OAuth callback, and the dashboard's link from such a record to its credential provider opened no provider, because the record did not name the provider's id. Both now work for new records; records written by earlier releases are shown only to appliance-wide callers.

## [1.0.7] - 2026-11-10

### Breaking

- **Refuse local x402 and MPP payment on surfaces** — A surface with an enabled local x402 or MPP payment policy now answers every request, over HTTP and over the fabric, with `503 payment_unavailable`, and the dashboard reports `payment_status: unsupported_legacy`. Saving such a policy, MPP auto-pay, or a Transit Point payment policy is refused with `400 local_payment_unsupported`. A stored `mpp_auto_pay` is ignored, so a 402 from a fabric Target reaches the caller. Charging again needs a payment gateway, and connecting one needs the `agent_pay_delegation` flag; the Payments page and the legacy payment history stay visible.
- **⭐ Upgrade every node on shared storage together, and do not downgrade** — Loading a stored `{"type": "x402", "provider": "agent_pay"}` policy rewrites the surface in the `agent_pay` shape, writing out the default triggers of its protocol when it named none, and older releases cannot read that record. Upgrade every Active and Standby node on one storage directory together, and keep a storage backup before upgrading: a downgrade loses those surfaces.
- **Turn the peers agent_pay policies name into payment gateways at startup** — At startup each peer that an `agent_pay` policy of a surface it may serve names becomes a payment gateway with exposure `none`, as Use as payment gateway makes one, so it no longer reaches local surfaces: it answers `404` on `/v1/gateways/*`, is managed under `/v1/payment-gateways/*` with `payment_gateways.*`, and cannot be turned back into a peer. A peer that a surface routes to over `fabric://` is left a peer with a warning, and the surfaces charging through it answer `503 payment_unavailable` until it is made a payment gateway; startup turns `agent_pay_delegation` on so that it can be, once those routes point elsewhere.
- **Replace input.payment.verified with the chargeable-request context** — Payment is now taken after policy, so OPA's `input.payment` is `{required, rail?, amount?, currency?}` from the trigger check and the manifest snapshot. A Rego rule that reads `input.payment.verified` no longer matches.
- **Refuse retyping a credential provider or deleting a secret that is in use** — Changing a credential provider's type to one that needs a user's consent while a surface uses it for Target authentication is refused with 409; a surface save that overlaps the type change is checked again under the same lock, so one of the two is refused. Deleting a secret that a surface, credential provider, A2A proxy or STS client uses is refused with 409 unless the request adds force=true; a save of one of those records that overlaps the delete and names the secret is either refused or seen by the delete, which then answers 409. The 409 body names the referencing records the caller may view (by tenant, resource scope and view permission) and counts the rest. Deleting a provider in use keeps its existing provider_in_use answer. Upgrade note: remove the references first, or delete a secret with force=true.
- **Refuse and stop sending stored Target credentials that break a header rule** — A surface save no longer keeps a stored Target or Transit Point Target authentication that breaks a header rule; only target_https and target_source_auth breaches the stored surface already had are kept. On every request the gateway checks the same header rules before it reads a secret or requests a token, on the direct path, a proxy:// MCP Target, Fabric send and receive, a Transit Point and the agent card fetch: a breaking credential is never sent, fallback reject answers 502 Target authentication failed, passthrough forwards the request without it, and either way a target_auth_failed record is written. The agent card preview reports it as invalid_header. The startup and reload credential policy report lists such surfaces under rule target_auth_header. Upgrade note: a stored Target authentication with an invalid or reserved header name (such as X-Transit-Token or Host), a header another credential on the leg sends, or a header format without exactly one {value} or with CR, LF or NUL is refused on its next save and no longer sent; check the startup report for target_auth_header and fix the header_name or header_format.
- **Stop serving MCP proxy and redirect prefixes that hide a dashboard page** — `mcp_proxies` prefixes and `redirect` routes with a target are now left out when they would hide a dashboard page, as channels and proxy routes are, since they are registered ahead of the dashboard in the same way. The dashboard pages that were not reserved before are now: `/access-tokens`, `/api-keys`, `/apikeys`, `/authorities`, `/cli-consent`, `/issuers`, `/jwt-verification-strategies`, `/payment-gateways`, `/policy-definitions` and `/sts-clients`. The whole first segment counts, so a prefix below a page, such as a redirect at `/settings/old`, is left out too. Clients of such a prefix stop reaching it until it is renamed; the gateway logs each one it leaves out.
- **Present the current mTLS certificate to Fabric peers after a rotation** — The identity the gateway presents to Fabric peers kept the certificate fingerprint, subject and issuer from when the identity was created, even after the stored certificate was rotated. It now carries the current certificate's details under the same DID, matching the certificate its request credentials already use. Fabric peers whose policy pins the old fingerprint deny requests after a rotation until they update the policy to the new fingerprint. Upgrade note: an identity whose certificate was rotated before the upgrade presents the new fingerprint on its first request after it; update peer policies that pin a fingerprint before upgrading.
- **Refuse saving a JWT Bearer method that names no audience** — A script or client that creates or changes an Access Point jwt_bearer method with an empty audiences list, without protected resource metadata, now gets a 400 from the admin API, and so does a dashboard save of a new JWT Bearer method with no Accepted Audiences. List the audiences the method accepts, or set audience_claim to client_id or azp and list the clients. Saving a client-bound method beside an on_demand, pre_authorize or elicit credential binding is refused too.
- **Point the consent_required authorization URL at the gateway's start page** — The authorization_url in a consent_required answer and in an MCP elicitation is now the gateway's {callback URL}/start page, not the vendor's authorization URL, so agents that checked it for the vendor's host, and vendor mobile-app handoff from that link, no longer work. Remedy: open the URL as given; a reverse proxy in front of the gateway must also forward {callback_path}/start.
- **Refuse a non-conforming or shared callback_url on authorization code providers** — Updating an oauth2_authorization_code provider without an issuer in a way that changes its redirect URI now needs an https callback_url with no query or fragment whose path ends in the provider's stored callback_path, and a redirect URI no other provider in any tenant uses. Two stored providers sharing a redirect URI, such as two tenants' providers with the same provider_id and no callback_url, both fail closed at consent, and the gateway logs an ERROR naming each pair by provider id at startup and reload. A stored provider whose callback_url breaks these rules, or whose authorization_endpoint or additional_params break the consent start rules, also stops getting consent links, and the gateway logs an ERROR naming its provider id and the rule at startup and reload. Remedy: give one of two providers sharing a redirect URI a callback_url on a distinct host, or recreate it, which gives it a path of its own, and register the new redirect URI with the vendor; correct the setting a rule ERROR names through the API or the dashboard. An update that keeps the stored redirect URI is accepted, so such a provider can still be edited. The dashboard's Credential Provider editor now keeps an existing provider's stored callback_url, reverse proxy prefix included, while its host is unchanged and it already conforms, and otherwise builds it from the stored callback_path, so a provider saved from the dashboard keeps a conforming path.
- **Build a new consent provider's callback path from its id, not its provider_id** — Every new credential provider gets the callback_path {oauth_callback_route}/{id}, the id the gateway generates for it, so an authorization code provider's redirect URI is its own and tenants can use the same provider_id without one refusing the other or learning that it exists. Creating an oauth2_authorization_code provider without an issuer with a callback_url is refused with 400, since its path is known only once it is created; set callback_url with an update. Providers stored before keep their {oauth_callback_route}/{provider_id} path and vendor registrations. The dashboard creates the provider, then saves its Callback URL from the returned path, and opens the created provider so the URL to register is shown; a provider without a callback_url shows the gateway's own Callback URL, read from the new gateway_base_url field of GET /v1/config/surface-routing. Remedy for API clients: create without callback_url, then PUT it ending in the returned callback_path, and register that redirect URI with the vendor.
- **Take surfaces naming another surface's API key out of service under enforce** — Under tenant_credential_isolation enforce, a stored surface whose Identity names another surface's API key, even one of its own tenant, is not ready after upgrade and answers 503, and saving it unchanged answers 400. A stored surface naming a key whose owning surface no longer exists is not ready under enforce too, as the key's tenant cannot be told. Under warn such a surface keeps serving and is logged as other_surface. The Surface Builder marks an Identity that names a blocking key. To fix such a surface, add an API key to it and save it naming that key, which gives the surface a new DID; update whatever is bound to the old one. To set up a new surface, create it, add its API key, then save it naming that key.
- **⭐ Refuse requests to a surface that names a revoked API key, under warn too** — A surface whose Identity slot, target identity injection, Transit Point or legacy Caller Context secret names a revoked API Key Provider key is not ready in both tenant_credential_isolation modes and answers every request with 503, on the direct path, over Fabric and on its Transit Points, with no agent card preview. Its DID is derived from the key id with no key lookup, so before, a revoked key kept its surface's DID and a stored surface naming one served under warn. This includes a key of a deleted surface: deleting a surface revokes its keys, and the gateway revokes the keys of surfaces deleted before the upgrade when it becomes Active. The node that revokes the key refuses the next request; other nodes reread the key's status from disk on the first request after cache_refresh_interval_secs, or after 30 seconds when it is 0. The refusal is logged with reason api_key_revoked. To find affected surfaces, read the credential policy report at boot or reload (revoked_key) and the WARN lines with rule="provider_key_tenant" and not_ready=true; per surface, api_key_references marks the key blocking and serving_status reads not_serving, and the Surface Builder marks an Identity that names it. To bring a surface back, select an active API key of that surface and save it. The surface then gets a new DID, since the DID comes from the key id and a revoked key can't be restored; update whatever is bound to the old one. Rotating a key keeps its surface serving.
- **Stop serving a stored surface that names an API key that no longer exists** — After the upgrade, a stored surface whose Identity slot, target identity injection or Transit Point names an API Key Provider key id that no key has answers every request with 503 under tenant_credential_isolation warn as well as enforce, logged with reason api_key_missing and reported as missing_key with not_ready true. Before, such a surface kept serving the DID of a key that was gone. A legacy Caller Context secret with no key of its id stays a plain secret. To keep such a surface serving, select an existing API key of that surface and save it, which gives the surface a new DID; the Surface Builder marks an Identity that names the missing key.
- **Refuse surfaces naming a key id that an unreadable key folder may hold** — While an API key folder cannot be read, a surface naming a key id that no loaded folder holds answers every request with 503, logged with reason api_key_store_unavailable, in either tenant_credential_isolation mode, since the unread folder may hold another tenant's key. Before, such a key read as missing and its surface kept serving. Surfaces whose key ids are all found keep serving; when the API key store itself cannot be read, every surface naming a key id answers 503. While a folder is unread, a save that adds such an id answers 503 "API key store is unavailable", api_key_references reports it unknown, tenant reassignment and its impact preview answer 409 while any surface names a key id, and boot and reload name the unread folders. A single key record that cannot be parsed or decrypted is dropped instead, and its key reads as missing (api_key_missing). Once the folder can be read, the surfaces serve again at the next check after cache_refresh_interval_secs (30 seconds when it is 0).
- **Refuse a new surface whose id still holds API key records** — POST /v1/surfaces with an id whose API key folder holds any key record, readable or not, such as the keys kept from a deleted surface, answers 400 "Surface ID '<id>' is not available" in either mode, and 503 "API key store is unavailable" when that folder cannot be read, so a new surface cannot take over another surface's keys and the DIDs derived from them. A client that deletes and recreates a surface under a fixed id is refused. To free the id, an unrestricted caller deletes each kept key with DELETE /v1/api-keys/{surface id}/{key id}; the create then answers 201, and the recreated surface gets new DIDs from its new keys.
- **Refuse a gateway.json in which two entries serve the same path** — Two entries of `channels`, `mcp_proxies` and `routes` on one prefix made the gateway crash while building its router, or let one entry hide the other. The gateway now refuses to start with such a file, and a reload refuses it, naming each pair. A `redirect` beside a `proxy` route or an `mcp_proxies` prefix on the same prefix is still accepted, since one serves only the prefix and the other only the paths below it. Every prefix but a `redirect`'s is now read without a trailing `/`, so a channel at `/agents/` serves `/agents/foo`, and `/agents` beside `/agents/` is a clashing pair. Before upgrading, check `channels`, `mcp_proxies` and `routes` for prefixes that are equal once a trailing `/` is dropped.
- **Refuse to save a surface on a route callers cannot reach** — The admin API saved an active surface whose route no surface prefix served, or on a path another gateway.json entry serves, such as `/api/...` or a `redirect`, and callers never reached it there. It now refuses a new or changed route like that with `400`, naming the entry that serves the path or listing the surface prefixes, read from gateway.json as it is on disk. This holds for surfaces reached only over Fabric, or used only for their Transit Points, too: API clients that save them on such routes must move them under a surface prefix, which also moves the default paths of their Transit Points. A route an active surface already had is not checked again, so older surfaces can still be edited, and the gateway logs a warning at startup for each one callers cannot reach.
- **Refuse JWKS URIs resolving to loopback and send JWKS and token calls direct** — Saving a JWT verification strategy already refused a jwks_uri naming a loopback address or localhost. Creating or updating one whose host resolves to loopback, an unspecified address or cloud metadata now answers 400 too, and a stored one fails its JWKS fetches; credential provider token endpoints were already held to that rule. JWKS fetches and token requests also ignore HTTPS_PROXY, HTTP_PROXY and ALL_PROXY, since a proxy would resolve the host again, so a host that reaches the internet only through a proxy must allow direct egress to its identity providers; a startup warning names them.
- **Require the JWT verification strategy edit permission for the JWKS URI probe** — POST /api/v1/jwt-verification-strategies/validate-jwks-uri needed only jwt_verification_strategies.view, which let a read-only user make the gateway fetch arbitrary URLs. It now needs jwt_verification_strategies.edit, the permission a save needs, and a personal access token needs that scope.

### Security

- **Keep gateway permissions away from payment gateways** — `gateways.*` never reaches a payment gateway and `payment_gateways.*` never reaches a peer: `/v1/gateways/*` and `/v1/payment-gateways/*` answer `404` for the other kind, and `/v1/connection-points/*` answers `404` for, and never lists, a payment gateway's connection points, so they cannot be deleted, disabled or reconnected there. A payment gateway reaches no local surface, and its exposure cannot be widened.
- **Let a paired connection point's mediator account admit only its peer** — A paired Connection Point (OOB responder or acceptor) now sets its mediator account to explicit-allow with only its peer's DID on the access list and anonymous receipt off, instead of admitting every sender. The peer is the secure DID recorded at pairing, else the DID on its gateway record; with neither the account stays open and a warning names the Connection Point, so an older pairing keeps working. Invitation endpoints, pairing temporaries and legacy Connection Points stay open. The periodic account check and the Gateway Ping repair restore each Connection Point's own ACL rather than opening it.
- **Keep payment credentials away from Targets, policy input and logs** — A payment credential in a header, tool argument, `_meta` or A2A message metadata is removed before the MCP and A2A context that policy, the policy input log and every later stage read is built, and before the request is forwarded, whether or not it is charged, so neither a Target, an MCP proxy backend nor a log receives one.
- **Forward a principal chain only for a verified agent** — A principal chain a client supplies is forwarded to agent-pay only when this gateway verified the caller's agent DID and the chain ends at it, and the audited payer is that DID or a hash of the authenticated principal made with `AG_IDENTITY_HASH_PEPPER`.
- **Ignore unsolicited x402 verify responses from peers** — This gateway sends no x402 verify request, so a peer's `x402/verify-response` is now dropped instead of creating a verified record in the x402 transaction store.
- **Throttle forced payment manifest refreshes** — `GET /v1/payment-gateways/{id}/surfaces/{surface_id}/payment-manifest?force_refresh=true` asks agent-pay at most once every 10 seconds per manifest, serving the cached one with `refresh_throttled` in between, and at most 4 requests wait on payment gateways at once, the others getting `429 payment_manifest_busy`.
- **Refuse a connection point DID request whose id is not a single path segment** — `GET /.well-known/connection-points/{cp_id}/did.json` and `did.jsonl`, and their `/connection-points/...` forms, now answer `400` when `cp_id` is not a single path segment inside the connection points directory, as the trust registry and issuer DID routes do.
- **Take the patched actix releases for four advisories** — The gateway reaches `actix-web` and `actix-http` through its OpenAPI MCP support, and four advisories published against them on 6 November are fixed in releases the project already allowed: RUSTSEC-2026-0448 and RUSTSEC-2026-0455, where a compressed request body could exhaust memory and CPU before any extractor limit applied, RUSTSEC-2026-0447, the same through a different path, and RUSTSEC-2026-0446, request smuggling through malformed chunked request trailers. Only the lock file changed: no dependency was added or removed from `Cargo.toml`, and taking the patched releases moved a handful of transitive pins with them.
- **Bind a grant through an MCP Proxy only to the provider's resource** — A grant goes through a Transit Point's MCP Proxy only while that proxy is of the surface's own tenant, served only through surfaces (direct_access false), and its base_url is a public https URL with the origin of the provider's resource, and only while the Transit Point speaks MCP, sends the grant in a header and has no target_auth. The rules are checked when the surface is saved, when a connect starts (the whole Transit Point rule set), when the grant is released and again with the proxy the call is sent through, so a proxy edited afterwards to point elsewhere is never sent the grant; the tool call itself goes only to that origin, through pinned Strict egress with redirects refused. A grant sent in a custom header is marked sensitive, as an Authorization header is. The Access Point's Target is still never an MCP Proxy with a grant.
- **Pin a grant through an MCP Proxy to the proxy a surface save approved** — Whoever may edit an MCP Proxy, but not the surface, could change which operation a tool name calls on the grant's origin, its description or the server name, and so what every person's grant is used for and what the agent reads. Each proxy:// Transit Point's external binding is now pinned to the proxy's grant_pin (transit_credentials.mcp_proxy_pin). A save that sends the proxy's current grant_pin pins the binding to it; one that sends none, or sends the stored pin back as the dashboard does, keeps the stored pin, so a save made for another reason (a PATCH, a variant change, a tenant transfer, a dashboard save) never approves an edited proxy; one that sends any other pin is refused (400, mcp_proxy_pin_not_current, naming the Transit Point); and a new binding is pinned to the proxy as it is. While the proxy differs, or a binding stored outside the API has no pin, the Transit Point answers nothing through it: connect start answers 409, telling the operator to save the surface with the proxy's current grant_pin, and initialize, tools/list and every call connector_unavailable, each refusal with a log line naming the Transit Point and the proxy; the save answers a Warning header. The pin is compared only with a proxy of the surface's own tenant, and a save fails (500) rather than drop a pin when the proxy cannot be read.
- **Refuse a grant through an MCP Proxy whose spec overrides the HTTP method** — A header parameter named X-HTTP-Method-Override, X-HTTP-Method or X-Method-Override, or a query parameter or declared request body field named _method or one of those, in any case, lets an agent ask many REST APIs and web frameworks to run another method than the operation's, turning an allowed tool into another behind the same tool name. A proxy:// Transit Point with a grant binding is refused when the surface is saved (400), when a connect starts (409) and when a tool call is sent (connector_unavailable) while its MCP Proxy's spec has a tool with such a parameter. A tool call carrying the grant whose body has a top-level key of one of those names, declared or not, is refused when it is sent and never reaches the backend. An MCP Proxy's name, the tool names it logs and the MCP method a caller sends are now logged escaped, the method cut short.
- **Pin credentialed legacy MCP proxy tool calls to the vetted backend address** — A legacy MCP proxy tool call that carries the Target credential, direct or over Fabric, now vets its REST backend at call time with the policy the proxy's base_url was saved under and connects only to the vetted address, as a 2026-07-28 tool call does. A backend host that later resolves to loopback or cloud metadata, as a DNS rebinding host would, fails the call with "REST tool target is not permitted" and gets no request and no credential. Calls without a credential are unchanged. Upgrade note: a stored legacy MCP proxy whose base_url resolves to loopback no longer receives credentialed tool calls.
- **Fail a caller who sends a second token under another spelling of its header** — JWT Bearer caller authentication now treats `_` and `-` in the token header name as the same header, as some servers do: a second token under another spelling, such as X_User_Token beside X-User-Token, fails the caller, and every spelling but the verified header is stripped before forwarding, directly and over Fabric, so an unverified token no longer reaches the Target.
- **⭐ Refuse Target authentication settings that could leak or inject headers** — A surface save is refused with 400, naming the field, when a new or changed Target authentication on a Target or Transit Point uses an invalid or reserved header name (including headers the gateway sets itself, such as the transit token and caller identity headers), a header another credential on the same leg already sends, or a format without exactly one {value} or with CR, LF or NUL. Header names are compared without regard to case or to _ against -, as the request path compares them. A credential sent to a Target or Transit Point endpoint that is not https, or from a leg that does not check its callers, is refused only while target_https or target_source_auth is enforce; in warn it is saved, logged and answered with a Warning header. The same caller rule holds an A2A or AP2 surface whose a2a-proxy:// Target sends its A2A proxy's Direct Line credential. A stored setting that already broke a rule still saves while that leg is unchanged, and a surface may not have two Transit Points with the same id. Upgrade note: API clients that create or change a setting with a bad header name or format now get 400.
- **Drop caller headers that spell the Target credential header with _ for -** — A caller header named like the Target authentication header was already withheld on the direct Target path, Fabric receive and Transit Points when the names matched without regard to case. It is now also withheld when it spells the name with _ for -, which some servers read as the same header, and on two more legs: an Access Point's Fabric send and the agent card fetch that sends no credential. So a caller cannot supply or override the Target credential on any leg. Upgrade note: a client that sent its own value under such a name with fallback passthrough now reaches the Target without it.
- **Drop caller headers named like a delegated credential header** — An Access Point, including its Fabric send and its agent card fetch, and the Fabric receiving gateway no longer forward a caller header named like the bearer_header (Authorization) or custom_header of a credential binding in the surface's outbound_credentials, compared without regard to case or to _ against -, whether or not the binding adds a value to that request, as a Transit Point already did. So a caller cannot send X_Vendor_Token next to the gateway's X-Vendor-Token. Upgrade note: a caller value under such a name, including an Authorization header kept by forward_header on a surface with a bearer_header binding, no longer reaches the Target.
- **Keep the Target credential under the Target's base path** — The gateway sends a Target credential only to URLs with the Target's scheme, host and port whose path equals the Target path or lies below it, compared after decoding percent-encoded unreserved characters and removing dot segments (an encoded slash is never decoded). A request that would leave the base path, such as /route/%2e%2e/admin, gets 502 Target authentication failed and is not forwarded. This covers direct forwards, an Access Point's Fabric send, the Fabric receiving gateway and MCP proxy tool calls; a GET or HEAD of the surface's configured agent card location stays allowed. Upgrade note: clients that relied on paths leaving the Target base path on a surface with Target authentication now get 502. Paths a server could still resolve outside the base, such as ..;/, ..%2F, ..%5C, ..%3B and their double-encoded forms, are refused as well, and a 2026-07-28 MCP proxy tool call refuses a path parameter that would form one.
- **Never follow an MCP proxy redirect with the Target credential** — A legacy MCP proxy tool call that carries the Target credential is now sent by the gateway's own REST client, which does not follow redirects; the previous client followed them and could carry a credential header to another host. A redirect now fails the tool call. Calls without a credential are unchanged. Upgrade note: credentialed legacy tool calls now take the gateway's REST client instead of rmcp-openapi's, and return the same tool result for a JSON response, failure statuses included. They apply the mcp_http size limits, encode each path parameter as one segment, refuse a base_url with user info, and turn a 2xx response the tool's output schema does not admit into a tool error. A non-JSON result's text leaves out the response headers, a non-UTF-8 body other than an image fails, and a refused call names its cause in different words. They reach the same URL as before: a base_url without a trailing slash keeps its last path segment.
- **Withhold Target credentials from non-https or unchecked legs when enforced** — Each forwarded request checks its Target credential against the target_https and target_source_auth rules. With warn (the default) the credential is still sent and a warning naming the surface, its surface variant, and the Transit Point on its leg, is logged once per leg (the base surface and each variant are separate legs) and rule until restart, and only when the credential is sent. With enforce the credential is withheld and its fallback decides: reject answers 502, passthrough forwards without it. A Transit Point is judged by its own endpoint and caller check. An Access Point with at least one caller_authentication method checks its callers, however many methods it has; a caller carrying credentials for more than one method fails authentication and gets no Target credential. The gateway's own agent card fetch has no caller, so only target_https can withhold its credential. The Direct Line credential of an a2a-proxy:// Target's A2A proxy is held to target_source_auth only: with enforce, on a surface whose Access Point checks no callers, it is withheld and the request goes on without it. Upgrade note: with enforce, stored http, fabric:// or proxy:// Targets and Transit Points lose their credential under target_https, and legs without caller authentication, including A2A proxy Targets, lose it under target_source_auth.
- **Keep a Transit Point's credential under its destination's base path** — A Transit Point request that carries the Transit Point's Target authentication credential is refused with 502, and not sent, when its path would leave the destination's base path after unreserved percent-encodings are decoded and dot segments removed. This applies to http(s) and fabric:// destinations. Upgrade note: Transit Point requests whose path climbs out of the destination's base path now get 502 instead of being forwarded with the credential.
- **Update actix-web and actix-http to patched releases** — The locked actix-http 3.13.1 and actix-web 4.14.0 (pulled in through rmcp-actix-web) are now 3.21.0 and 4.19.0, which fix RUSTSEC-2026-0446, -0447, -0448 and -0455: request smuggling through malformed chunked trailers and unbounded decompression of request bodies. The dependency audit passes again.
- **Build rmcp without its Streamable HTTP client transports** — RUSTSEC-2026-0462: an rmcp Streamable HTTP client buffers a response body with no bound, so an MCP server it calls can exhaust the gateway's memory. The gateway asked for rmcp's `client`, `client-side-sse` and `transport-streamable-http-client` features and used none of them: it serves MCP through rmcp-openapi, and the Streamable HTTP client it calls MCP endpoints with is its own, on eventsource-stream and reqwest. Those features are now off, so the affected transport is no longer compiled into the gateway. The advisory is recorded in `.cargo/audit.toml` with that reasoning, because the published fix is rmcp 3.9 and rmcp-openapi 0.28.1 still requires rmcp 1.x; it comes off that list when rmcp-openapi moves on.
- **Encrypt storage at rest in new local and Docker environments** — Environments the development setup creates kept secrets, OAuth tokens and DID private keys in plain JSON, because their generated config.toml had encryption at rest off. make config-certs now also generates envs/master_encryption_key, and a config.toml that the setup creates turns encryption at rest on: a local environment reads the key from that file, and a Docker one from AG_MASTER_KEY, which make docker-run passes from that file and docker-compose.yml passes through. Export it with export AG_MASTER_KEY=$(cat envs/master_encryption_key) before docker compose up, and keep the file, since storage encrypted under it cannot be read without it; if it goes missing while encrypted storage is left under envs/, make config-certs stops instead of generating a new key. Existing environments keep their setting, and a regenerated config.toml keeps the [encryption] table of the one it replaces; an unencrypted one opts in by setting [encryption] by hand. The output of --generate-bootstrap is unchanged, so deployments built from it are not affected.
- **⭐ Store sessions under a hash of their token; everyone signs in again once** — After upgrading, every dashboard user signs in again and every DID Auth caller authenticates again: startup deletes the session files earlier versions saved. Dashboard sessions and DID Auth sessions were saved as files named by their bearer token, with the token also inside the file, so anyone who could list the sessions directory, a backup or a copy of it could use any live session, an administrator's included. Encryption at rest did not help, since it encrypts what a file holds, not its name. Each session is now stored under the SHA-256 hash of its token, which cannot be used to sign in, and a presented token is hashed to find its session. An earlier version cannot use the new files, so rolling back also signs everyone out. See docs/SOURCE_AUTH.md.
- **Refuse new JWT Bearer methods that accept tokens for any audience** — An Access Point JWT Bearer method with an empty audiences list skipped the audience check, so a token its identity provider issued for any other service authenticated the caller it named. The admin API now refuses a new or changed method without an audience, on the surface and in variants, unless the Access Point publishes protected resource metadata, which binds the audience.
- **⭐ Bind each OAuth consent callback to the browser that confirmed the start page** — Consent now starts on a gateway page that shows the agent, surface, provider and scopes. Its POST, accepted only as the person's click on that page (same-origin, with Sec-Fetch-User and Sec-Fetch-Dest showing a navigation they started, or the page's Origin from a browser without fetch metadata), sets a per-consent __Host-ag_consent_<id> cookie (Path=/, no Domain, Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax) and seals its hash into the state, so a forwarded or leaked callback or vendor URL opened in another browser is refused with 403 before the code is exchanged. A start link is used once per gateway process; the page itself has no side effects, so link scanners do not use it up.
- **Keep the consent binding cookie away from agents, targets and peers** — Every inbound and outbound listener now takes any ag_consent cookie off a request before it is relayed, and strips any ag_consent Set-Cookie the gateway did not write from a relayed response, so a managed agent, target or Fabric peer never sees the binding cookie and cannot plant one in the person's browser.
- **Check the OAuth consent callback's provider, state and iss before the exchange** — The callback now refuses, before any token request, a path that names another provider than its state, a provider that changed or gained an issuer, a state that is not a callback state, and a missing or different iss from a provider with an authorization_response_issuer. The token request sends exactly the redirect_uri the authorization request sent. A failed token exchange now uses up the state, as it uses up the code, where before the same callback could be retried. Every refusal shows one fixed page with no-store and no-referrer that reflects no vendor or error text, and is logged with a reason; one that carried a valid state is also audited as delegation_failed.
- **Refuse unsafe authorization endpoints and reserved additional_params** — The vendor authorization URL is built only for an https authorization_endpoint with a host and no credentials or fragment, whose query and additional_params hold at most 32 parameters and repeat none of the ones the gateway sets, such as state, redirect_uri or code_challenge. Creating such a provider, or updating one to such an endpoint or additional_params, is refused with 400. A consent link is not minted for a stored provider that fails these checks; the binding is unavailable instead.
- **Revoke a surface's API keys when the surface is deleted** — Deleting a surface now revokes its active API keys and keeps their records, so they no longer authenticate callers and the deleted surface's id stays tied to them. The surface's key folder is read from disk, so keys another node created are revoked too. Each revoke is logged per key id with actor surface-delete and is permanent. When the folder cannot be read or a key cannot be revoked, the delete still succeeds and its response carries an api_keys_left_active warning saying which keys stay active. A surface still naming such a key stops serving until it names an active key of its own, which gives it a new DID.
- **Keep API key usage apart from the key record so it cannot undo a revoke** — An API key's last use is now written to its own file beside the key record, at most once a minute per key and node, and never into the record, so recording a key's use on one node can no longer write a revoked key back as active on any node. A failed usage write is logged and the caller is still accepted. The API key list and detail show the usage file's time, or a record's own last use from before this change. A key revoked on another node stops authenticating here at this node's next cache refresh, or with cache_refresh_interval_secs at its default of 0, at restart or promotion to Active.
